#2749. Nvidia's billion-dollar board bet: Who's protected as lawsuits mount?

Jensen Huang is back as CEO of Nvidia, after President Donald Trump urged the company's board to rehire Huang. But investigations into the company, its executive leadership and its board continue as outside criticism mounts.

"Clearly they caved to external pressure," said Betsy Atkins, an author and longtime public-company director. "The board failed," she said, when it initially allowed Huang's removal. "The board's role is to think of all stakeholders."

California Attorney General Rob Bonta is investigating whether Nvidia misrepresented information to investors. "There is classic corporate securities law on the books in California that prevents investors and the public from being misled by companies," Bonta said on CNBC's "Squawk on the Street" on Tuesday.

Directors are already defendants in a shareholder lawsuit accusing them of failing to oversee Nvidia. A separate securities class action targets the company and Huang, its co-founder.

Now the board’s insurance choices are part of the story. A risk advisor with direct knowledge of Nvidia’s coverage, who asked not to be named because they are not authorized to speak publicly about client matters, said the company built an approximately $1 billion D&O tower, insurance jargon for directors and officers insurance policies from different companies that combine to cover one massive risk.

Nvidia "loves insurance," the advisor said. “They buy insurance as if they have no balance sheet.”

A billion-dollar tower would essentially mean Nvidia "bought the market," purchasing all the coverage it could get, according to another industry expert, who has 25 years working in financial insurance. The expert asked not to be named due to sensitivity surrounding relationships with past or potential future clients.

But Nvidia directed all that coverage to Side A only, according to the risk advisor. Side A protects directors and officers personally when the company cannot indemnify them. Side B covers the company for legal defense costs or settlements on behalf of directors and officers, and Side C protects the company when it's named as a defendant in a suit such as a securities claim.

The risk advisor says Nvidia believed it had "the war chest" to protect itself against a securities class action suit.

That choice is raising questions about whether the board protected itself at the company’s expense. CNBC asked Nvidia for comment but received no response.

An insurance CEO, who asked not to be named in order to protect client relationships, characterized a billion-dollar policy as a lot of coverage, but said a Side A-only allocation was a surprising move because it results in imbalanced coverage, protecting only directors.

That structure is not automatically a governance failure, according to the D&O insurance expert with long experience in coverage for banks, drugmakers and other businesses that often face litigation. Side A-only programs are common among companies with the capital to manage the corporate risk internally.

"For a company with Nvidia’s resources, retaining the company-side risk can make financial sense," said the industry expert with 25 years working in financial insurance. It's Side A D&O that's "imperative to corporate governance."

"It is a capital-allocation decision," the expert said. The company decides that buying insurance for its own balance sheet is not worth the premium. In other words, Side B and Side C are considered too expensive for the coverage the company gets.

The question for shareholders is whether the board’s decision to hold that risk was a sound use of the company’s resources and whether the company can absorb the costs if the claims grow.

A shareholder derivative complaint filed Nov. 11 names Huang, directors and other officers. It alleges that they failed to oversee risks involving Huang’s relationship with Megaspeed executive Alice Huang and made misleading statements. It also alleges that some defendants sold roughly $2 billion in Nvidia stock while holding material nonpublic information. Those remain allegations, not findings.

A separate securities class action, filed Nov. 9, names Nvidia and Huang. It seeks damages for investors who bought Nvidia shares between Oct. 9, 2025, and Nov. 8, 2026, alleging the company and Huang misled investors.

The distinction matters: The derivative suit is brought on Nvidia’s behalf, while the class action seeks recovery for investors.

"Insurance is already at work," said Michel Léonard, chief economist at the Insurance Information Institute, describing standard policy mechanics, not Nvidia’s specific policy. "Once the suit is filed, D&O starts paying defense costs, long before anyone proves anything."

A claim still has to meet the policy’s terms, and defense costs can reduce the remaining limits. Nor does a drop in Nvidia’s market value by itself establish an insurable loss. Plaintiffs would need to prove legally recoverable damages; the policy would respond only to covered losses.

The board has already taken steps it says are aimed at accountability. Nvidia announced an independent investigation on Nov. 8, and independent directors retained outside counsel two days later. Huang resigned as CEO and a director on Nov. 11; the board appointed CFO Colette Kress interim CEO. Nvidia said it had no reason to believe national security or company secrets had been compromised by Huang's extramarital relationships.

Atkins, who joined the board of Wynn Resorts in 2018 after founder Steve Wynn resigned, said allegations are not conclusions and that boards should also weigh governance concerns against the value a founder brings to a company’s innovation. Nvidia's investigation is continuing even as Huang resumes leadership.

“Will companies’ hyper-accelerated innovation speed be the same without them? I don’t think so,” said Atkins, author of "Don't Waste a Board Seat: A Decision-Maker's Guide to Choosing Directors Who Create Value."

Nvidia’s 2026 proxy also says the company has indemnity agreements with its directors and officers — separate from its insurance. Those agreements provide for the company to cover certain costs and liabilities when the law permits.

Curry, 38, has missed the Warriors' past seven games with the abdominal injury, At the time, the Warriors announced that their star guard would be sidelined for at least two weeks before being re-evaluated. So this latest update approximately follows that timeline.

The two-time NBA MVP has begun individual on-court workouts and is making good progress based on Friday's evaluation, according to the Warriors' release (via the San Francisco Chronicle's Luca Evans).

Following Friday's game, the Warriors host the Los Angeles Clippers (4-14) on Sunday and the Utah Jazz (3-17) on Tuesday. Curry will be re-evaluated the next day.

#2739. Semiconductor chips are the new oil. Here's why

The artificial intelligence boom has pushed semiconductors past oil and gas in global export value for the first time, according to a Deutsche Bank analysis of International Trade Centre data.

Yet the milestone arrives at an awkward moment, just as investors reassess the outlook for artificial intelligence spending and drive down the price of semiconductor stocks. Last week, before Monday's breathtaking reversal, semiconductor stocks suffered their worst weekly loss in a quarter century, with the PHLX Semiconductor Index (SOX) plunging nearly 11% last Friday alone, capping a more than 17% weekly decline.

Nvidia soared more than 31% Monday after the leading maker of artificial intelligence chips had fallen for a seventh straight week, plunging 46% during that stretch. Then it fell again, dropping almost 10% on Tuesday and Wednesday. Advanced Micro Devices tumbled 20% in two days last Thursday and Friday. Micron Technology sank 27% over the seven days ended last Friday.

"For decades we've talked about the petrodollar. Perhaps increasingly we need to think about the chip dollar," Jim Reid, global head of macro and thematic research at Deutsche Bank wrote in a report this month.

Semiconductor exports overtook oil and gas for the first time during the January-March quarter of 2026, reaching about $1.61 trillion, compared with $1.53 trillion for oil and gas, the report said, using four-quarter rolling totals.

The gap widened in the second quarter, when semiconductor exports totaled about $1.60 trillion and oil and gas exports reached about $1.47 trillion. Disruptions to oil supplies through the Strait of Hormuz after the Iran war helped widen the gap. Daily oil flows through the Strait collapsed nearly 77%, to 4.9 million barrels in the second quarter of 2026 from 21.6 million barrels in the fourth quarter of 2025, according to the U.S. Energy Information Administration.

Yet the demand for semiconductors has proven insatiable in the face of soaring data center construction, boosting global trade in the increasingly scarce AI component. Hundreds of billions of dollars of orders for graphics processing units and data centers aim at providing AI computing power, Rothschild & Co. Redburn wrote in a report last Thursday.

"Compute will be the new commodity fueling this transformation on a global scale," Rothschild analyst Cara Thomson said.

The shift toward chips has macroeconomic implications just as the explosion in oil trade did in the 1970s.

Oil-exporting countries historically recycled much of their surpluses into the purchase of U.S. Treasuries and other bonds, helping finance U.S. deficits and underpin demand for other government debt.

Reid at Deutsche Bank said that since semiconductor-surplus countries in Asia are more closely tied to U.S. tech companies, some chip-export earnings could flow back into U.S. technology stocks, startups and data centers, further helping to finance the AI investment cycle.

The changing dynamics in global trade could also tilt the mix of global financial flows, Reid said. "So welcome to the chip-dollar era: potentially more growth, more excitement, more volatility, and structurally higher yields" in corporate and government debt.

#2736. Mike McCarthy just 'focused on winning' after Aaron Rodgers saga

Mike McCarthy helped guide the Pittsburgh Steelers to a resounding 27-5 victory over the Denver Broncos on "Black Friday" after one of the franchise's most tumultuous weeks in recent memory.

The trouble began after the Steelers announced veteran quarterback Aaron Rodgers was being benched in favor of Mason Rudolph at 11:10 p.m. ET on Tuesday, Nov. 24. The following day, Rodgers did not report to the Steelers' facility and instead aired his grievances with McCarthy in an appearance on "The Pat McAfee Show" before Pittsburgh released the 42-year-old quarterback.

The accusations claimed McCarthy had fallen asleep in team meetings and had performed a "1, 2, 3, Packers!" during a halftime huddle breakdown with his team, among other issues. Naturally, it garnered significant attention in the NFL world, with many decrying Rodgers for breaking the sanctity of his team's locker-room bond.

And one of the most notable reactions came from McCarthy's predecessor, Mike Tomlin, who referred to the organization as having a "dumpster fire" on its hands following Rodgers' release.

Following the Steelers' Week 12 win, McCarthy was asked for his reaction to those comments.

"I really don't have one," he replied, stoically, during his postgame news conference. "Let's just be real here. There's only so much time and energy in the day. I think you have to, when you go through things like that, I have to put all my energy into winning."

"I get that this is part of the responsibility and the opinions and comments of others is why I'm in here to answer questions," McCarthy added. "But I'm focused on winning."

McCarthy's approach paid off, as the Steelers were able to improve to 7-4 with their victory over the Broncos. That has put them a half-a-game behind the Cincinnati Bengals in the AFC North race while giving them more breathing room in the wild-card race, as they now are a game-and-a-half ahead of the No. 8 seed New England Patriots.

McCarthy appreciated earning that victory after what he acknowledged was a difficult week for his team.

"It was definitely a hard travel week for us as a football team," he said. "I'm proud of our players, I'm proud of our coaching staff, I'm proud of our support staff and I really appreciate all the other support."

The Steelers were playing at home at Acrisure Stadium in Pittsburgh in Week 12, so it wasn't immediately clear whether McCarthy was using the phrase "hard travel week" metaphorically as a reference to a series of events the team endured amid the Rodgers saga.

Either way, McCarthy came out of the win pleased with his team. He praised Rudolph for being able to effectively guide the team without Rodgers and also praised his veteran-laden defense for its stalwart performance against Bo Nix.

But above all else, McCarthy was grateful for the team's veteran leadership for establishing "urgency" after a meeting they held earlier in the week.

"I think it says a tremendous amount about the team," McCarthy said, referencing the win. "I really probably want to pivot off the leadership council meeting. Everything that was discussed was healthy. A lot of things in there, but what came out of it was urgency. And I thought our leaders did a tremendous job of bringing the urgency to their rooms and it definitely showed up."

"You definitely saw the urgency today," McCarthy later added. "That's what really stood out for me as far as the vibe in the locker room. Our guys do a good job of that. From the get-go, our leaders really had the guys going. And our defense got us started. We played the game that we needed to play. Just very, very impressed and very proud of the way we handled this."

#2734. Mark Pope reacts to brutal loss vs. Wichita State and the late inbound turnover disaster

After Kentucky fell 73-71 in overtime to Wichita State on Friday night, Mark Pope stepped up to the UK Sports Network radio postgame show with Tom Leach, knowing exactly what the first question was going to be.

Trailing by two with 5.4 seconds left in overtime following a Wichita State turnover, Kentucky had a chance to go the length of the floor.

Instead, Alex Wilkins passed the ball along the baseline to Mason Williams, an illegal play off a dead-ball turnover that immediately handed possession back to the Shockers. With the Wildcats dropping to 4-3 before December, fans are rightly calling for blood, while Pope is asking for patience.

Pope did not dodge the mistake, calling it "brutal" to be the reason your team loses.

"That was a misread on my part," Pope told Leach. "That clearly was costly, very frustrating; we will get better. We have a play card for special situations, and I just went to the wrong space."

Even with the sting of a self-inflicted wound at the worst possible moment, Pope emphasized that his roster is not folding under the early-season pressure.

"It's a tough thing to fail, and we will go back to work tomorrow, and we will get better," Pope said. "We have a group that wants to fight."

That fight didn't translate to rebounds Friday night.

While the botched inbounds play will dominate the headlines, the game was really lost on the glass long before overtime. Playing without Malachi Moreno, Kentucky was dominated on the boards, getting outrebounded 55-36 overall and 23-12 on the offensive glass.

In fact, Dillon Battie and Will Berg outrebounded Kentucky by themselves, 37-36. Pope pointed straight to that physical gap as the defining theme of the night.

"Give credit to Wichita State," Pope said. "We got outrebounded by 19, and that was a big part of the game. It is a place we have been focused on. We have to find ways to do this with a reduced lineup. That is a place we will continue to put a massive amount of focus."

He summed it up even more bluntly moments later.

"We got out-physicaled tonight," Pope said. "That was the story of the game."

There is no time for Kentucky to dwell on a frustrating home loss. The Wildcats have to regroup quickly before hitting the road to face Virginia next Wednesday in the SEC/ACC Challenge. When asked what his message is to the locker room heading into Charlottesville, Pope pointed back to the fundamentals that betrayed his team in the second half.

"There are a lot of things we need to focus on," Pope said. "We got outrebounded by 20; we continuously fouled around the rim in the second half. I do think we will find ways to get better in those areas."

Accountability in the postgame booth is one thing, and Pope certainly owned the night's biggest mistake. Good for him.

But with a tough road test looming and Big Blue Nation's patience wearing thin, the only thing that will quiet the noise is seeing those words actually translate onto the floor.

#2729. 3 Observations from Indiana men’s basketball’s 93-57 win over Bowling Green

Indiana men's basketball dominated Bowling Green tonight, going on a run just over five minutes into the game that effectively put the Falcons to bed. The Hoosiers were shooting well, defending well, and rebounding well in what may have been the most complete performance from this group so far.

Though it wasn't a marquee win like last week's victories over Arkansas or Kentucky, the margin of victory was one that boosted Indiana's computer profile. KenPom only favored Indiana by 20 entering the game, so the blowout moved Indiana up five spots in the national ranking.

At 8-0, Indiana looks much improved over Darian DeVries' first season, with KenPom now projecting the Hoosiers to win 23 games and go 12-8 in Big Ten play.

Indiana had a season-high 18 assists tonight against Bowling Green, starting to resemble the fast-paced offense we saw from DeVries' group last year. While Indiana has more players that are capable of creating unassisted buckets, the ball movement tonight helped the Hoosiers take more quality looks from the perimeter, something that will be important for maintaining a respectable 3-point percentage this season.

Bryce Lindsay's deep shot against Arkansas will go down as a high-point of the season, but over the last couple games Indiana had gotten into the habit of chucking a few too many heat checks from deep or taking transition threes that negate the team's rebounding advantage.

Seeing the way the ball moved tonight was an encouraging step for this group not just cause it made the offense look a little better, but because it reflects well on the team's chemistry so early in the season. With a team full of transfers, having nine different players record an assist in a game is no small feat.

Aiden Sherrell had one of his stronger performances of the season, finishing with 16 points, nine rebounds, two blocks and an assist. He also went 2-6 from 3-point range.

Tonight's performance was a good example of why he's a potential NBA player. A seven footer who can score from the perimeter, rebound on both ends of the floor, and protect the rim is a valuable piece for any team. After an impressive year last year at Bama, he's averaging more points and shooting better from deep this year at Indiana.

Even though he cooled off by the end of the night, his early threes are exactly what Indiana will need from him going forward. Establishing himself as a threat at the beginning of the game will force defenses to account for him until the final whistle.

It's hard to say enough good things about Bryce Lindsay so far this year. Against Bowling Green, he scored 18 points, grabbed five rebounds, had three assists, and also added two steals. He was also super efficient, going 2/4 from 3-point range and making all of his free throws.

What I like most about Lindsay's game is his ability to be what DeVries needs on the floor, no matter who he's playing with. He can play point guard alongside Darren Harris at the shooting guard spot, or play shooting guard next to Markus Burton.

Calling him the sixth man doesn't even feel right, as he plays the fifth most minutes on the team, but he's been a huge asset for DeVries and Indiana this year, doing everything asked of him, and then some.

#2726. CKB Teammate Wants Carlos Ulberg & Navajo Stirling to Fight

UFC lightweight Dan Hooker wants to see his teammates at City Kickboxing, Carlos Ulberg and Navajo Stirling, fight for the 205 lbs belt.

Earlier this week, CKB head coach Eugene Bareman said that Ulberg and Stirling would not fight each other, even if the UFC light heavyweight title is on the line, because the two men are "brothers."

But if you ask Hooker, their teammate at CKB, the two men should scrap it out for the belt.

Speaking to Ariel Helwani, Hooker said that, if it were up to him, Ulberg would fight Stirling for the belt at 205 lbs.

"I'm from a pretty rough-and-tumble gym, let the boys play. That's where I'm at, but I already said that. I was like, man, I'd bash Oscar (Willis) for 50 bucks and a pack of smokes. I'm not taking anyone's opinion into account, but Eugene, I'm not sure what he'll do. He probably will not want the boys to compete against each other," Hooker said (via MMAjunkie.com).

Bareman has made it clear that he does not want to see his two star light heavyweights fight each other, but ultimately, the UFC matchmakers are the ones who make the fights, so in the end, it's the UFC's decision.

Even if Ulberg and Stirling wanted to fight each other, Hooker said that the final decision comes down to what coach Bareman wants.

"It's not like we have a vote or anything, it's kind of just down to Eugene. But that's the thing: If Eugene says they're not fighting, is everyone going to accept that? But man, what if he doesn't want them to? He doesn't want to put himself in that position where he's having to choose between two of his fighters," Hooker said.

"At the end of the day, for a coach, it's an incredible problem to have that you've built a champion and a contender head and shoulders above everyone else that the fight needs to happen. As a coach, it's about the best problem you could be facing."

#2725. It’s time for Basketball: San Antonio Spurs vs Los Angeles Lakers

Welcome to the Game Thread. Veterans of the Game Thread know how we do things around here, but for all you newbies we have a few rules. Our community guidelines apply and basically say be cool, no personal attacks, don't troll and don't swear too much.

The Spurs lost their first road game and NBA Cup game of the season on Wednesday night, in a 111-106 thriller in front of an enthusiastic Moda Center crowd as both Dame and Deni Avdija went nuclear and out-executed the Silver and Black down the final stretch. Some missed free throws, a lane violation and an 8 second violation were costly as the Spurs ran the gamut of rarely enforced reasons to lose points at a key juncture of the game. The Spurs missed having De'Aaron Fox available, as his quickness and steady administration of the offensive sets was missed, especially in crunch time. Dylan Harper's absence was apparent, too, with the Spurs were missing his ability to get points even when the offense was not creating any space. The Spurs became too reliant on three point shooting, which isn't good for them, because only Champagnie is a good three-point shooter among the rotation players, and everybody in the Moda Center knew that, especially the Trail Blazer defenders. The good news is that Fox should be back in tonight's lineup. The better news is that Harper is listed as questionable on the injury report, which means that he could return to play tonight, just 10 days since fell on the court and injured his hip with less than a minute left in the Pistons game. Harper's injury was initially estimated to keep him out from 1-3 weeks (or 2-3 weeks, depending on where you read the news) and he's definitely on the lower end of that estimate.

Tonight, they face the Los Angeles Lakers in their final game of the Emirates Cup group stage. The math is pretty simple. In order to advance to the knockout rounds, they will have to beat the Lakers tonight. Because the Trail Blazers won on Wednesday, they will win the group and automatically advance if they win tonight's game against the Warriors, which tips off at the same time as the Lakers/Spurs game. In that case, a win for the Silver and Black would send them to the quarterfinals as the group winners, with a home game on December 4 or 5. If the Blazers win tonight, then the Spurs would probably advance to the semis as the Western Conference wild card, because they would have the best point differential among the second place finishers, barring some unlikely scenarios like the Grizzlies defeating the Timberwolves by 40+ or the Nuggets routing the Jazz by 50+ [OK, the second one could happen]. If the Spurs are the wild card, they will play on the road against the top team in the west, which would probably be the Wolves or Mavericks.

Is that all clear? Probably not. But if the Spurs lose tonight, it's even simpler. They will join the also-rans and have two more games added to the schedule in the second week of December against other non-qualifying teams. Although it's probably marginally better for the Spurs overall record, having two games against mediocre opponents added to the schedule than having to play two teams against top four teams in the conference, it would be a disappointment to a team that's looking to prove that they're ready to compete for the NBA title again this season. Last year, the top two teams in the NBA Cup were the teams in the NBA Finals, and that could happen again this season.

A lot of times, you can refer to the best player on the opponents' team as "the head of the snake." That's not exactly true of Luka Doncic. He's not the head of the snake, he's the whole damn snake. His usage rate is insanely high, and the rest of the team is pretty much relegated to watching him work on the offensive end. If the Spurs can force him to give up the ball early, and make the Lakers run the offense through someone else, especially if it's not Austin Reaves [questionable with a bruised heel], then the Spurs can hold down their scoring. The Lakers defense is decent, but the Spurs offense will be fine if they move the ball and don't just settle for three point shots. Let's GO SPURS!! Castle must dominate Luka.

#2722. Mike Macdonald on Sam Darnold, when healthy: "I think he's done a good job"

Quarterback Sam Darnold is back as Seattle's starter this week, after missing two games due to quad and rib injuries. It's an opportunity to improve on what has been a challenging start to the 2026 season.

On Friday, coach Mike Macdonald was asked to assess Darnold's performance when healthy.

"I think he's done a good job," Macdonald said. "As a football team, we've been good enough to go 6-4, and we want to get better. Yeah, he's right there with everybody else."

Well, he's not right there with the player who replaced him. Drew Lock has started three games and played all but one drive of another. His record is 4-0. Darnold is 2-4.

And their numbers are starkly different. Darnold has thrown 10 touchdown passes and nine interceptions. Lock has thrown seven touchdown passes and no interceptions.

Lock's passer rating is 125.6. Darnold's is 79.5.

Lock's games have come against the Patriots, the Cardinals twice, and the Raiders. Darnold has faced the Commanders, Chargers, 49ers, Broncos, Chiefs, and Bears — a much tougher slate of opponents.

The Seahawks won a Super Bowl with Darnold. That counts for something. Still, they're 6-4. They have an opportunity to get a seventh win on Sunday, with Brock Purdy out for the game against the 49ers.

#2719. Victor Wembanyama makes MVP statement in Spurs' comeback win over Lakers

The roars of Frost Bank Center in the closing moments of the San Antonio Spurs' 99-96 win over the Los Angeles Lakers on Friday, Nov. 27 could've applied to two players on the floor at the time. After all, that's been the biggest debate of this young NBA season -- who between Victor Wembanyama and Luka Dončić is more of the favorite to be the league's Most Valuable Player five months from now?

But there was no mistaking who was the recipient of those chants on Friday. Spurs fans were going crazy for one of their own as Wembanyama capped off a 20-point comeback with a free throw to secure a 99-96 win over the Lakers, a spot in the NBA Cup quarterfinals and early bragging rights over Dončić.

It wasn't a bad night for the Lakers superstar; he finished the night with 31 points, six rebounds and eight assists. Nineteen of those points came in the first half as LA took a 16-point lead into the locker room. But once the teams came back out for the third quarter, it was all San Antonio.

The Lakers were outscored 61-42 by the Spurs over the last two quarters, powered by Wemby's 24 points, 16 rebounds and six stocks and 26 points from Stephon Castle.

"We just said we gotta find our energy, be on our front foot, which we weren't in the first half," Wembanyama said on NBA Nightcap postgame. "... New challenges every time, every season. We handled that one the right way."

The clash of MVP frontrunners lived up to its billing on Friday. So did the game's overall billing as the game of the year so far.

It was the two best teams in the Western Conference, with the two best players, with their supporting casts at full strength after Austin Reaves, Dylan Harper and De'Aaron Fox all returned from injuries. And for the Lakers, it answered a much-debated litmus test in regard to their strength of schedule. It's one thing to get to the top of the West by beating up on the Sacramento Kings and Utah Jazzes of the league, but the Lakers showed they can hang with – and at times look dominant against – the defending Western Conference champions.

This game was winnable for the Lakers, which makes the loss and the 17 turnovers all that much more frustrating. Things don't get any easier from here, however. They'll continue their road trip on Sunday against the No. 3-seeded Memphis Grizzlies before heading straight to Houston for the second night of a back-to-back against the fourth-place Rockets.

The Spurs will take on a slate of Eastern Conference opponents in the Boston Celtics, Milwaukee Brewers and Toronto Raptors before facing the Dallas Mavericks in the NBA Cup quarterfinals on Friday, Dec. 4.

The Lakers rallied at the end, but ran out of possessions as Mitch Johnson smartly had the Spurs foul down the stretch to prevent LA from having a chance to get the three they needed to force overtime in the closing seconds. The Spurs clinch their spot in the NBA Cup quarterfinals and supersede the Lakers for the top spot in the Western Conference.

The battle of MVP frontrunners was as advertised; Victor Wembanyama bounced back from a frustrating first half and finished the night with 25 points, 16 rebounds and six stocks while Luka Dončić scored 31 with six rebounds and eight assists.

The Lakers can't buy a bucket right now. The Spurs have open up an eight-point lead – their largest of the night – midway through the fourth after outscoring LA 13-5 since the end of the third. The Lakers have not made a field goal in almost two minutes; in that span, they've turned the ball over three times.

The Spurs have come alive in the second half. It was a chaotic third quarter with sloppy turnovers on both sides, but it's been San Antonio who had the upper hand, outscoring LA 30-14. They came out the gate on a 9-0 run that snatched the momentum in the early minutes to cut into the Lakers' 16-point halftime lead. Luka Dončić has 25 points but the Lakers as a whole missed 10 of their last 11 shots down the final stretch.

Victor Wembanyama jammed home the putback on a missed free throw from De'Aaron Fox to cut the Laker lead down to five at the 2:05 mark. Fox later hit a floater with 38 seconds left, and a three from Keldon Johnson knotted the game up at 68-all, erasing LA's lead heading into the fourth quarter.

Complete domination by LA in the first half. Luka Dončić has a game-high 19 points and is scoring almost at will to go along with five rebounds and three assists. And while Victor Wembanyama has been limited offensively due to head coach JJ Redick's clear gameplan of keying in on the 7'6 big man, but he still has five rebounds and four stocks (steals and blocks combined into one stat) with eight points.

Reaves has struggled early in his first game back, missing each of his three attempts taken in the first quarter. So it's only fitting that his first points in the last five games came on a ridiculously tough shot.

Towards the end of the second quarter, Reaves dribbled out to the wing to avoid a one-on-one with Victor Wembanyama. When Devin Vassell switched onto him, Reaves immediately slashed to his left towards the paint and into the low post, stepped back and let it fly as he faded away with Wembanyama helping back late.

The ball swished through the net to give the Lakers a 19-point lead over the Spurs.

LA has gotten off to an early lead by forcing chaos in the paint, doubling Victor Wembanyama and forcing six Spurs turnovers. Luka Dončić is leading the way with 14 points for the Lakers on 6 of 13 shooting, though he's yet to hit a three in six attempts. Austin Reaves is still searching for his first points in his return to action.

#2718. 5 questions with a Chargers expert: What to expect from D.J. Uiagalelei?

On paper, the New England Patriots and Los Angeles Chargers are quite familiar with one another: they last met in January, in the wild card round of the NFL playoffs. However, there have been quite a few changes since that game, especially at the most important position on the field.

Tommy DeVito will start at quarterback for New England in place of Drake Maye, while Justin Herbert's absence as Chargers QB will allow D.J. Uiagalelei to get his first career start. To learn more about him, and the rest of the Chargers in 2026, we spoke with Michael Peterson of Pats Pulpit's sister site Bolts From the Blue, the SB Nation community for all things Chargers.

1. With Justin Herbert missing this game due to a concussion, what can you tell us about backup D.J. Uiagalelei's strengths and weaknesses?

So D.J. Uiagalelei has made massive strides since coming out of college. The fact he has been able to stay with a NFL team this long is truly a testament to that because he was — to put it bluntly — a horrendous passer to end his collegiate career. With a full year and more behind Herbert, mainly as the third-stringer, he's progressed enough to be a serviceable player for an emergency situation like this.

As a passer, he is fairly composed in the pocket and has enough athleticism to escape and extend plays. He also plays with some notable confidence and will not think twice about attempting a tough throw. However, he's not reckless with the football. He knows when to pull it down and get yards on the ground when possible. In his first NFL action in garbage time against the Texans, he helped bring the offense down the field and even threw his first professional touchdown on a great rip over the middle.

His biggest weakness will be that he's still a wildcard and an unknown in this league. He could be serviceable like I mentioned, but he could also flail in the moment like most young passers in their first career starts.

I think Mike Vrabel would be wise to turn up the heat early and often and hope the second-year UDFA crumbles under the pressure.

2. Why has it taken so long for Omarion Hampton to start breaking out, and what challenges does he present for a defense?

Hampton unfortunately did not help himself this year by beginning the campaign with back-to-back games where he lost a fumble. It also did not help that he lost those fumbles in pivotal moments that directly led to the Chargers losing said games. It unfortunately took a bit for him to get back into the god graces of Mike McDaniel and now he seems to be off and running with a renewed energy. He's been phenomenal and looks every bit the player the Chargers wanted to take in the first round.

Download the SB Nation app on iOS or Android.

As a runner, Hampton's skillset is not designed to make someone miss by being shifty. He's going to make them miss by running full speed through the weak point of your tackle attempt to consistently gain extra yards and fall forward at the end of runs. He's the type of runner that will get the most out of the minor creases given to him and when the defense is worn down by the fourth quarter, he'll look to keep pounding it even more. Patriots should come prepared to try and get him stopped before his momentum builds, not unlike how the Chargers kept Derrick Henry in check during their lone win of the season.

3. LT Joe Alt is a Pro Bowler, but the rest of the Chargers' offensive line consists of rookies and backups. Where are the non-Alt bright spots up front, and who do you expect the Patriots to try to exploit?

One guy who went from roster afterthought to solid starter the past few weeks has been rookie left guard Logan Taylor. He was a sixth-round pick out of Boston College this year and mainly was used at tackle during the preseason despite guard be the obvious place for him in the NFL. Once he settled in after being forced into the starting lineup, he's been one of the lone bright spots outside of Alt. Another recent bright spot has also been the return of Kayode Awosika who, after being the original starter at left guard before his fibula injury, is now starting at right guard over Cole Strange (who should have been benched a long time ago).

Both of the guard spots being reinforced has led to back-to-back strong rushing games for Hampton, as well as two consecutive intereption-less games by Herbert.

As far as which player up front the Patriots should exploit, it has to be rookie right tackle Travis Burke. As good as Burke has been in the run game, he is quite a bit behind as far as pass protection fundamentals. He is athletic enough to stay outside, but he lacks proper set points and punch mechanics. He gets beat by twitchier rushers and overall speed that can threaten his corner more than he likes. He is coming off a horrible game against David Bailey and the Jets and the Patriots should do their best to exploit that side of the line this week.

4. What has contributed to the Chargers' struggles stopping opposing passing games? Are there any defenders the Patriots should try to avoid?

Against the Jets, the absence of cornerback Tarheeb Still was felt, especially when injuries have continued to rear their head in a position group that is terribly shallow. Undrafted rookie Rodney Shelley has played a lot this season and his production has been abysmal. Even veteran Donte Jackson, who has battled multiple injuries this year, is still very feast-or-famine.

The absence of Derwin James has also been apparent. He's a back-to-back second-team All-Pro slot defender and anyone who knows ball understands how good an elite slot player can be for a defense. The Chargers have been getting by — for the most part — with a group of guys no one has probably heard of at the second and third levels. The Jets game was a bit of a fluke, but they'll have to right the ship fast to avoid making that result less of "who they are" and more of that fluke I just mentioned.

5. It seems there is some disconnect between Jim Harbaugh, Mike McDaniel, and Justin Herbert. What is happening with the Chargers' current leadership structure, and who do you see remaining in L.A. when the dust settles?

What some may not understand about Harbaugh's role as the head coach is that he is a true CEO. He does not have a hand in helping scheme either side of the ball. He makes his hires and then he allows how coaches to coach. That's a major reason why no coordinator has been fired or changed during the season under his leadership and it's the main reason why McDaniel was never going to be fired. There's no one to pick up the slack and Harbaugh sure wasn't going to step in and start calling plays for the first time in decades.

When it's all said and done, Harbaugh will most likely remain, even if he truly should not. The team will owe him a ton of money over the next two years and the Spanos family will not want to fork out money to a coach that's no longer working for him. Harbaugh's two 11-win seasons prior to this year still gives him some grace, but with how bad this year is, there will be no more grace for him should this disaster continue into the 2027 season.

#2713. Who’s got Cup fever?! Sixers dominate Pacers, win Group B, advance to knockout stage

The Cup continues to bring the best out of the game's greatest.

The Sixers handled the Indiana Pacers 111-87 Friday night. They advance to the Knockout Stage of the NBA Cup for the first time as a franchise, winning Eastern Conference Group B. They're next Cup game will be either Dec. 4 or 5.

LeBron James kept his hot hand, finishing with 21 points, seven rebounds and six assists shooting 8-of-13 from the floor. VJ Edgecombe went for nine points on 4-of-6 shooting along with five rebounds and seven assists.

Tyrese Maxey struggled from the field, shooting 3-of-13 for 13 points, but did also have six assists. Returning from a two-game absence due to illness, Joel Embiid had some rust to shake off, going 5-of-14 from the floor to finish with 14 points along with 11 boards.

Jaylen Brown was once again out for the Sixers with hamstring injury management, as was Dean Wade. Tyrese Haliburton and T.J. McConnell were out for Indiana.

This game started as ideally as it could for the Sixers. The fifth starter for the night was Dominick Barlow, who opened the scoring by cutting to the basket for an easy layup. LeBron buried a couple of threes, one just beat the shotclock with a hand in his face, before Barlow put back a Maxey miss to start the game on a 10-2 Sixers run. LeBron drilled his third attempt following a Pacer timeout but missed his heat check on the following possession.

There was a lot of violence at the rim early as Embiid tried to dunk on Kelly Oubre Jr. as he got open on a roll, but Oubre stuffed the attempt and gave a hearty celebration after. Trying to get down the floor quickly, the Pacers turned it over with a sloppy pass, as Maxey was able to get the ball out to Edgecombe who threw it down hard.

The center substitutions were very frequent early. Embiid's first shift was under five minutes. Adem Bona played for just 2:41 and made an impact in that time, pinning an Aaron Nesmith attempt against the backboard. Indiana opened the game 3-of-12 from the floor as a team.

It was good to see Labaron Philon Jr. back in the mix, but the Embiid-plus-bench lineup did not get off to a good start. Embiid allowed Jay Huff to swoop into the lane for an offensive rebound, turning that into a corner three. The Sixers called timeout, but were unable to stop the run as Jabari Walker got called for an offensive foul coming out of it. Nesmith got to the line to cap off a 9-0 Pacers push. Embiid knocked down a three and Simons put up a quick five to keep the Sixers up by eight after the first.

The Sixers' offense slowed down, taking nearly three minutes to score their first points of the second. It was another impressive LeBron bucket as he banked home a layup despite still trying to get his hands on Kentavious Caldwell-Pope's pass, but Indy kept creeping back on the other end.

Edgecombe hit a big three off an offensive rebound which forced a Pacer timeout, but the offense was still struggling to get back on track. Maxey didn't score his first points until halfway through the quarter, starting 0-of-3 with three turnovers.

It does seem like luck snow piles when your shot isn't falling. The offense had broken down into a lot of isolations, but Maxey also had a very open floater in the paint rim halfway down and out. On the next possession he took a deep, early three in the shotclock which also bounced around before falling out.

Nick Nurse called a timeout after the Pacers had cut the lead to one and was able to draw up something to get them out of the funk. A few screens got Walker an open corner three that he nailed. Embiid and Maxey went to their two-man game on the next two possessions, getting an elbow jumper for the former and a layup for the latter.

The Sixers had plenty of time to get their act together thanks to the Pacers shooting 2-of-17 from behind the arc in the first half. They got to the free throw line way more than the Sixers, but they would have been able to take a lead had they hit anything from outside. Ironically, it looked like their third of the night was falling right before the half, but Ivica Zubac got called for basket interference. That gave Maxey time for one more drive, getting to the line to give the Sixers an 11-point lead at the half.

Barlow's impact was felt inside when he was on the floor. He picked up his second block early in the third. A couple possessions after he did that, he grabbed a missed Embiid jumper and dunked it back over the head of a defender.

Offensively the Sixers got some easy stuff to start the half. Maxey and LeBron each got to the line and LeBron bully balled his way for an easy post-up. They got a little jumper-happy again once they got the lead up to 18 and went cold on their pull-ups. A nice cut from Barlow was rewarded by James to break that up and prompt another Pacers timeout.

Bona struggled a bit more in his quick third quarter shift, picking up his third and fourth fouls in about a 30-second span. The Sixers seemed to have even more leeway to mess around though. Just like how they shot from the first half from three, the Pacers opened the third quarter 2-of-17 from the field.

In 59 seconds of game time Simons hit as many threes as the Pacers had all night. He started it with a stepback off the dribble over his defender. After a timeout, he came off a screen for his second and hit another pull-up for his third. Maxey hit him with a crosscourt pass a few possessions later for another before he missed his heatcheck several feet behind the arc as the Sixers coasted to a 27-point lead.

Despite some other games making it not really matter, the Sixers were fighting for that point differential to start the third. KCP got on the board with a couple of baskets, Barlow picked up a couple more cutting layups, and Simons hit a running layup.

Philon might have checked back into the game a little later in the second half than some may have wanted, but it was nice to see him drill a three as well. The end of the bench did allow a quick 9-0 run that prompted an angry timeout from Nurse after a Philon turnover.

The Sixers' scoring drought that lasted over three and a half minutes was ended by Tacko Fall, who got a big round of cheers from the fans left in the arena upon doing so. Fall was able to front Huff and slam home a big dunk on the next possession to give the people what they wanted to see.

#2712. Lakers fade after strong start, blow 20-point lead in NBA Cup loss to Spurs

SAN ANTONIO — The Lakers held the Spurs to 14 points in the first quarter, the lowest quarter outing this season for San Antonio, and then built a 20-point lead in the second behind an all-around effort.

By the end of the third, that lead was gone.

Los Angeles fell 99-96 to San Antonio on Friday night at Frost Bank Center, dropping to 15-4 and eliminated from NBA Cup contention, finishing group play at 2-2

The Spurs improved to 16-4 and advanced to the quarterfinals as the Western Conference wild card after Portland won West Group C.

"We'll keep getting better, that's the goal of this season. Hopefully we can become a great team," coach JJ Redick said.

Luka Dončić scored 14 points in the opening quarter, matching San Antonio's output as the Lakers took a 27-14 lead. L.A. added another 27 points in the second and went into halftime up 54-38 after shooting 51.2% from the field.

The Lakers still had a 16-point lead in the third quarter when Stephon Castle made it a two-possession sequence without letting them get the ball up the floor. He finished through contact, made the free throw, then jumped the ensuing inbound pass and buried a jumper. JJ Redick called timeout, and a 61-45 lead was down to 11.

The Spurs kept finding points after misses.

Victor Wembanyama turned a missed De'Aaron Fox free throw into a putback as part of a nine-point, six-rebound third quarter, and Keldon Johnson buried a 3-pointer in the closing seconds to tie it at 68 as the Lakers went scoreless in the final three and a half minutes.

San Antonio had outscored the Lakers 30-14 in the quarter.

The Lakers shot 34.5% from the field after halftime and went 12-for-42 from deep for the game. Luka Dončić finished with 31 points, six rebounds and eight assists, but his slump from 3-point range continued. He went 1-for-13 against the Spurs and is shooting 20.3% (13-for-64) over his last five games.

Austin Reaves gave the Lakers a chance to recover. Back after missing five games with a left heel bruise, he knocked down back-to-back 3-pointers to cut the deficit to two with 3:16 left. He finished with 21 points, eight rebounds and seven turnovers.

Then another missed Spurs free throw cost the Lakers. Fox ran down Wembanyama's miss in the final minute, and Castle stepped back for a 3-pointer with 43 seconds left to put San Antonio up 96-90. It was his third 3-pointer of the fourth quarter.

Reaves hit another 3 after Matisse Thybulle forced a turnover, and a Fox giveaway gave the Lakers the ball back down three. San Antonio fouled Jake LaRavia before the Lakers could get a tying shot up. LaRavia made both free throws with 7.7 seconds left, and Fox answered with two of his own.

The Spurs fouled again, this time sending Quentin Grimes to the line. Grimes made the first and intentionally missed the second, but Wembanyama grabbed the rebound. He split a pair of free throws, and Reaves' heave fell short at the buzzer.

Castle finished with 26 points and nine assists, and Wembanyama added 25 points and 16 rebounds. San Antonio scored 20 second-chance points. Walker Kessler had 10 points and 13 rebounds, including eight on the offensive glass, and Collin Sexton scored 12 off the bench for L.A.

The Lakers next face the Memphis Grizzlies on Sunday at 1 p.m. PT.

#2706. Horizon rushes by Notre Dame Prep to reach 5A final

Just when it seemed like the game was slipping away from Horizon, sophomore running back Dylan Brush came up big — just like he has time and time again this season for the Huskies.

Brush was the sparkplug in Scottsdale Horizon's 28-21 comeback win over Scottsdale Notre Dame Prep in the 5A semifinals on Nov. 27, scoring two touchdowns to help lead the team. Horizon (10-2) advances to the state championship game at Mountain America Stadium on Dec. 4 against Scottsdale Desert Mountain.

After trailing 14-0, the Huskies clawed their way back to come out on top behind a ferocious rushing attack.

"They're resilient," Horizon coach Tyson Ditmore said. "We've preached that the second half and third quarters are going to be really big for us this year. We came back out, relied on a run game. The kids, they were a little bit more cool, calm and collected. Really proud of the way that they bounced back."

Before Horizon could blink, it was already down 7-0. Notre Dame senior Matt Higby returned the opening kickoff 80 yards to put the Saints up just 12 seconds into the game.

After a quick three-and-out from the Horizon offense on its first drive of the game, Notre Dame responded with a 13-play, 79-yard scoring drive powered by the legs of running back Decker Haskins-Smith. Haskins-Smith, who had 12 runs on the drive, capped it off with a two-yard run to go up 14-0 with 3:23 left in the first.

Horizon's next drive ended up in catastrophe as a fake punt attempt was blown up by Notre Dame lineman James Mariotti, giving the Saints the ball at the Huskies' 36-yard line. But the defense held and forced a field goal attempt, which was blocked by Ryan Rottkamp and returned to Notre Dame's 32-yard line by Garrett Kerr, the first of a few big plays from Kerr on the night.

Just as the Horizon offense was getting into a groove, a tipped pass from quarterback Kai Knudson landed in the hands of defensive back Mason Pavone for an interception. But again the defense held firm and forced a punt to get the ball back.

That's when Brush began to put his stamp on the game. First, it was a 20-yard run to make it 14-7 with 2:33 left in the second quarter. The score provided much-needed life for a stagnating offense.

"We were leaning on him," Ditmore said. "We knew that the runs were going to open for him, and he did an awesome job of just continuing to get downhill. He got us that touchdown to put us right back."

After Brush got the offense going again on the first drive of the second half, he limped off the field after a hit on the sideline. Garrett Kerr entered and immediately popped off two big runs of 20 and 14 yards to get down to the one. Following a penalty from Horizon, Knudson punched it in from six yards on an option run to make it 14-13 following a missed point-after attempt with 7:04 left in the third.

But Notre Dame responded with another 13-play scoring drive, traveling 82 yards in seven minutes to bring the lead to 21-13 with 53 seconds remaining in the third. Haskins-Smith, the only source of offense the whole game for Notre Dame, scored on a two-yard run.

Brush came back on the field on Horizon's next drive and got the Huskies into the red zone before finishing it off with a two-yard touchdown run. Knudson connected with wide receiver James Klopfenstein — who returned after an eight-week absence — for the two-point conversion to tie it 21-21 with 7:57 left in the game.

Then came Notre Dame's only mistake of the game, and it was a costly one. A fumble from Notre Dame was recovered by defensive back Sam Klopfenstein, giving Horizon the ball close to midfield.

On back-to-back drives, it was the Klopfenstein brothers making a difference. The family started a legacy at the school with their older brother, Matthew, who is now at Baylor and played on the team that went to the 2021 title game.

"A family that's been at Horizon for God knows when," Ditmore said. "We wish that they had some more coming through, but Sam is the youngest. James went out and he finished his home game here on his own. Really proud of him. He's going to be back for us, ready to go again."

Two more big runs from Brush pushed the ball into Notre Dame territory. Facing a fourth-and-one and with the game on the line, Ditmore and his staff changed it up — going with Kerr for the play-call instead of Brush. Kerr rushed up the middle gap before bouncing on the outside for a 35-yard touchdown to take a 28-21 lead with 3:01 left in the game.

"He's a senior, it's his dream," Brush said. "We were so close last year to go to state; we lost by a field goal. This time it was amazing. It was right up A gap. He bounces outside, straight line, scores it. We knew we were gonna win. Well, we still (had) a little bit left, but it helped our hopes. I love Garrett Kerr. He puts in so much work throughout the whole year."

A sack from defensive lineman Vedder Wolfely backed up Notre Dame on its next drive. The Saints nearly completed a fourth-and-25 conversion attempt, but the pass was ruled incomplete. Horizon took a knee to end the game, setting up a rematch with the only team other than Notre Dame to beat them this season.

"I would love to have Desert Mountain because they ended our season last year," Brush said. "We want to end their season. We're gonna be ready for them."

It was a bitter end for Notre Dame (9-3), which surprised many by making it this far.

"No one really thought we'd be here," Notre Dame coach George Prelock said. "I think a lot in the preseason thought we'd go 3-7 at best. These boys grinded. We proved a lot people wrong. We really played as a team and came so close. I'm so proud of these boys."

#2704. John Harbaugh’s wife perfectly sums up NFL Thanksgiving Day drama

John Harbaugh could run for mayor of what his wife calls "Crazy Town."

Harbaugh will coach for his 200th career victory (regular season plus playoffs) Sunday against the Colts in Indianapolis. He ranks third among active coaches behind mentor Andy Reid (315) and Sean Payton (202), though only the last six wins are with the Giants.

"The wins are really hard to get," Harbaugh said. "They're really valuable. … There's no greater football joy than that winning locker room. I would just say every one of those wins is the same. It's like my dad says, 'You've never had a better win than this one.' Every [win] feels that way. The next one is really hard to get."

Ingrid Harbaugh started referring to the NFL as "Crazy Town" this year because of all the games decided by twists and turns in the final minutes.

The three Thanksgiving Day games all featured game-winning scores in the final three seconds. The Eagles' 21-point comeback against the Cowboys had the biggest impact on the NFC East-leading Giants — and serves as a great example of "Crazy Town."

New York Giants head coach John Harbaugh speaks at a press conference when the New York Giants practiced Wednesday, November 25, 2026 at Quest Diagnostics Training Center in East Rutherford, NJ. Robert Sabo for NY Post

"Oh, my gosh, was it ever," Harbaugh said. "It just goes to show you that these games are going to come down to the fourth quarter. You give the Eagles a lot of credit for the game they played. All six of those teams are fighting in the fourth quarter for the win.

"Kind of goes to the other point … how hard it is to win these games. And, if you can, become a team that finds ways to win games and can keep their poise and make plays. Hopefully, you scheme some good plays up, too, to win games at the end. That's the way they go. You have to be expecting that, I think — not be surprised by it."

Head coach John Harbaugh of the New York Giants is all smiles during the second half at MetLife Stadium, Sunday, Nov. 22, 2026, in Brooklyn, NY. Corey Sipkin for NY Post

DT Shelby Harris (illness) will not play after not practicing for the third straight day.

The Giants will ask more of Darius Alexander, C.J. Okoye and Bobby Jamison-Travis next to D.J. Reader.

CB Dru Phillips (illness) and OLB Abdul Carter (calf/illness) were limited participants but are expected to play.

Could Carter's workload be limited after his breakout 12-pressure game?

"He seemed OK today," Harbaugh said. "I'm sure we'll be monitoring that, but he seemed good today. He seemed strong."

The Jameis Winston quarterback sneak package isn't going anywhere.

When the Giants benched Winston (after four starts) to go to J.J. McCarthy, they kept Winston involved by having him run the Tush Push and other versions of sneaks.

"It was something he had been repping already, and so it was one less thing on J.J.'s plate," Harbaugh said. "Just being honest, I'd probably say that was kind of where we started with it. We kind of got to the point where, if it ain't broke, don't fix it. It's been good for us. It ends up being a whole package for us."

#2702. The Raptors showed they can win tonight: even if it’s just the Nets

To borrow words from Bret Easton Ellis' American Psycho (1991), there is an idea of a Toronto Raptors, some kind of abstraction, but there is no real team, only what they look like on the floor that night. But against the Nets, the Raptors looked like the type of team that they've billed themselves as since last season. Big, dangerous, able to guard well, while hitting their shots inside and outside of the arc.

And tonight, with Collin Murray-Boyles, Scottie Barnes, and Allen Graves all playing solid minutes, it looked like Vision 6'9" come to life, like a monster animated with the sole purpose of clobbering the Brooklyn Nets.

The first quarter had the Raptors shooting — and more importantly, making — more threes than usual. They outshot the Nets by both accuracy and volume, with Barnes and Graves looking like a real one-two punch. Julius Randle, while delivering thundering dunks, went 0-3 from three in the first quarter, as the Raptors ran a solid transition offence. The Raptors left the first quarter up four points on the Nets, despite Brooklyn dominating in the painted area, scoring 16 of its 27 points there.

Raptors fought themselves into a double digit lead over the Nets with an Allen Graves three, and continued to build momentum as the second quarter wound down. Scottie Barnes took it to the rack with a spin move into a filthy left hander that left the room begging for more. The Nets heated up from three by the end of the second quarter, but the Raptors maintained their lead into halftime.

The third quarter saw Immanuel Quickley rain in treys, as the Raptors' lead continued to grow. They cracked 100 points as the seconds rolled down, up more than 20 points. At the end of the quarter, Barnes drove to the basket and laid it in, cracking 30 points with 12 minutes left in the game.

By then, Barnes had not grabbed a single rebound, with his teammates doing a lot of the dirty work down low. Further, while Scottie generated great looks, it was Quickley who picked up double digit points and assists in the contest. Against a bad team, Barnes was able to focus on scoring, anchoring the Raptors' offence as they blazed past the Nets. The idea central to the Raptors' building around Scottie had, for the past two seasons, been to pair the young forward with a more veteran scorer.

However, this season has allowed Barnes to prove that on a nightly basis, he can take the top defensive or offensive assignment for the team. Barnes' ability to switch has long been lauded, but the offensive versatility he has displayed is something that the Raptors would benefit from remembering when Kawhi Leonard returns.

The Brooklyn Nets are the Brooklyn Nets, and using a hot knife to cleave through butter isn't a good test to see if your knife is actually sharp. But, it's nice to see after the Milwaukee debacle that the Raptors still have it in them to stomp the NBA's bad teams. More than that, however, tonight showed a version of the Raptors with high highs and low lows, with much to be emulated, and some things to be firmly rejected.

TORONTO, CANADA – NOVEMBER 27: Julius Randle #30 of the Brooklyn Nets dunks against Collin Murray-Boyles #30 of the Toronto Raptors during the first half of the Emirates NBA Cup group stage game at Scotiabank Arena on November 27, 2026 in Toronto, Canada. NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and or using this photograph, User is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. (Photo by Mark Blinch/Getty Images) | Getty Images

The shooting, especially from those who aren't usually snipers, was great. Allen Graves getting the minutes he needs to develop, and shining in them, produced some great highlights. But, moments of awkwardness, especially the Nets powering through the Raptors in the paint, were something that did not befit a team that projects a defensive mindset as outwardly as Toronto does.

While Ja'Kobe Walter was forced to leave the game with an injury, and did not return, he was quiet from the field, scoring his only points from the charity stripe. Similarly, Jaminson Battle ended the game with 1-6 from three. Sometimes, the shots just don't fall, but the Raptors shouldn't need to compromise between their shooters having good games from range.

Tonight's numbers from the Raptors looked great, but as much as the Raptors won this game, the Nets also lost it, giving up victory to the Raptors rather than having it taken from them forcibly. The rebounding difference between both teams is not something the Raptors will likely be able to sustain against more poised teams, nor the three point volume, but tonight's efficiency is what the team should model, and strive for, especially in harder matchups.

#2698. Steelers locker room 'prideful' but muted after win amid Aaron Rodgers firestorm

PITTSBURGH – Pat Freiermuth found himself in the center of the mess involving the Steelers and now-former quarterback Aaron Rodgers this week.

Rodgers told ESPN's "Pat McAfee Show" that the veteran tight end was the player who informed him coach Mike McCarthy was benching him in favor of Mason Rudolph late Tuesday night, Nov. 24.

Rodgers went on to say a lot of other explosive things about McCarthy, general manager Omar Khan and owner Art Rooney II over the course of his rambling 34-minute interview that captivated the NFL world.

Required reading: T.J. Watt, Steelers defense make statement in first game without Aaron Rodgers

You know the themes by now. Rodgers said McCarthy was sleeping in meetings and slacking on his film study. He called McCarthy unhealthy and unfit to be calling the offense for a team in the heat of a race for a playoff spot in the AFC. Et cetera, et cetera.

It was noteworthy, then, when Freiermuth was one of the players to address the situation most directly in the locker room after his team cruised to a 27-5 victory against the Denver Broncos on Black Friday afternoon at Acrisure Stadium.

"Pretty tough. I'm not going to lie. It was pretty tough," he said when asked how he's felt the past couple of days. "A lot of emotions. Just really, just sad. It was just a sad situation. We've just got to move on. Aaron's still one of my closest friends, but we just have to continue to move on and keep winning."

Most of his teammates tried to stay away from it, obliquely referencing the difficulty of the preparation amid a national firestorm while trying to keep the focus on what happened on the field.

That emotion was palpable, though. The room cleared out pretty quickly. Post-game victory music lasted maybe 10 minutes between the end of the game and media arriving for post-game interviews. Right tackle Brock Hoffman wasn't even sure who'd received the game ball, if one was awarded. There were few hoots and fewer hollers.

Not quite the scene you'd expect after what should have been a rousing home victory against one of the top teams in the conference. One the Steelers could see again in the postseason as they seek their first playoff win since 2016.

"Prideful" was the word Freiermuth used to describe how he was feeling about the result.

Earlier in the week, he'd delivered a fiery speech to the offense. He's one of the unit's most tenured players, with most of six seasons in Pittsburgh under his belt. He was glad to see his group respond with some explosive plays that complemented two defensive touchdowns in a game that was never really close.

"I was feeling passionate about what was going on," he said. "It was more from the heart of what the Steelers mean to me, and to my family and the Rooney family. And, just in general, nobody cares what we're going through at all, so we're either going to act soft about it or go out there and do something about it."

Hoffman found the comments "motivating" and felt they added energy to what was left of the short week of practice after the bombshell of the Rodgers news had landed, then settled.

The team rallied around quarterback Mason Rudolph, who completed nine of his 15 passes for 142 yards and a touchdown with no interceptions in Rodgers' place. The running game added 117 yards to control the clock.

"Obviously, the whole world was laughing at us," Hoffman said. "I'm not going to comment on that situation, but it felt like everybody was kind of counting us out. And for us to come in here, still stay undefeated at home and beat a really good Broncos team? You can't write that any better."

A turning point this week was what coach Mike McCarthy called a "players council" meeting after Rodgers was benched, where many parties apparently spoke freely about what was and wasn't working for the team that had been mired in a two-game losing streak against Cincinnati and Philadelphia.

McCarthy had touted it as productive in responding to Rodgers' comments, but few players really had their chance to tell their side of the story.

Freiermuth said he appreciated it while implying, as Rodgers explicitly said in his interview, that some of it was overdue.

"We were able to have grown conversations," he said. "We were able to hold each other accountable and talk to each other about what we want to fix. Or what we can do better as a team, as players, as coaches. Really impressive about the conversations we've had this week. The asks, the wants that coaches and players see. And it was just great to put it together in a win and have those tough conversations. Because at the end of the day, when stuff happens and you talk it through, you're able to get closer."

It's therefore possible that these Steelers are fixable. They now sit at 7-4, a half-game behind the Bengals in the AFC North standings.

Their schedule down the stretch is manageable. Jacksonville is the only remaining opponent with a winning record, so there's a runway for this team to position itself well if it can continue to come together.

Freiermuth pointedly stopped short of praising McCarthy, however, when asked how it felt to deliver a win for him personally. That felt noteworthy, given his proximity to Rodgers, and indicated that there's probably quite a bit of healing still to come in a locker room that seems to lack buoyancy, even if the most recent result feels satisfying.

"Obviously that's tough for him and his family," Freiermuth said of the criticism McCarthy has faced in the wake of Rodgers' comments. "We just wanted to win in general."

#2697. Knicks have no late-game magic as they’re eliminated from NBA Cup title defense

Captain Clutch had a chance to tie the game with three free throws after Ryan Conwell's boneheaded foul on Brunson's corner 3-pointer with 9.8 seconds left. But Brunson's first free throw clanked off the rim — a rare shortcoming for him in a big moment.

Brunson made the next two, and the Knicks trailed by one. But they weren't able to foul on the subsequent Heat possession, as Pelle Larsson's dunk put Miami back up three with 7.2 seconds left. Brunson — while trying to draw another foul — missed a 3-pointer on the other end, and the Knicks' six-game winning streak came to an end as they fell 108-105 to the Heat on Friday night at Madison Square Garden.

Miami Heat forward Giannis Antetokounmpo pulls down a rebound away from New York Knicks guard Josh Hart on Friday, Nov. 27, 2026, at Madison Square Garden. JASON SZENES FOR NEW YORK POST

"Very," Brunson said when asked how frustrating it was to miss the free throw.

Friday's loss meant the Knicks were officially eliminated from the NBA Cup. They needed to win by at least seven points and get a Pacers win over the 76ers to advance. Neither happened.

Brunson's missed free throws came on the possession after James Wiseman similarly came up short from the line.

Trailing by two with 36.8 seconds left in the game, the Knicks needed a stop and seemed to have bottled up Giannis Antetokounmpo, who tried to throw the ball off the backboard to himself. But Karl-Anthony Towns fouled him on the play.

Antetokounmpo missed both free throws, however, and Bam Adebayo fouled Wiseman going for the rebound after the second miss. Wiseman could have tied the game with 12.5 seconds left by making both. He made the first, but his second free throw rimmed out.

So, the Knicks overcoming a 23-point deficit was all for naught.

New York Knicks guard Jalen Brunson reacts to referee Scott Foster after a call at Madison Square Garden in New York, Friday, Nov. 27, 2026. JASON SZENES FOR NEW YORK POST

"We did not have a great shootaround today with our focus," coach Mike Brown said. "We have to be better in our shootarounds, coming into games — especially games like this. It was evident with how sloppy we played."

There will be no defense of the Knicks' NBA Cup title. They didn't even make it to the knockout rounds after going 1-3 in the group stage.

But being eliminated could end up a blessing in disguise. Brown — even before his team was knocked out — said that this time around, he values the extra rest the Knicks will get by not advancing in the tournament. And with how banged up they are, he's probably right — the Knicks (12-7) were without OG Anunoby (left calf strain), Miles McBride (sports hernia), Jordan Clarkson (right plantar fasciitis) and Andre Drummond (concussion protocol) on Friday. Towns played but has been battling an abdominal ailment.

Miami Heat forward Giannis Antetokounmpo pulls down a rebound away from New York Knicks guard Jalen Brunson on Friday, Nov. 27, 2026, in New York. JASON SZENES FOR NEW YORK POST

Now the Knicks will get easier opponents (other teams who were eliminated) and play just two games — one home and one on the road — over a nine-day stretch in the beginning of December. Advancing would have meant better opposition (other teams who advanced) and the potential for up to three more road games if they reached the Final.

Friday was their first matchup against the Heat since Miami acquired Antetokounmpo over the offseason. And the Greek Freak delivered his first 20-20 game for the Heat with 22 points and 23 rebounds. Adebayo, his partner in crime in a dominant frontcourt, had 25 points and seven rebounds. Towns recorded his 19th straight double-double to start the season with 19 points and 10 rebounds, but he couldn't take on the Giannis-Bam duo by himself.

"One guy has the second-most points in a game ever and the other guy, he's an MVP, a former MVP," Towns said. "It goes back to execution."

Brunson had a game-high 27 points — but the Knicks needed 28 out of him.

The Heat like to utilize a zone defense with their dominant frontcourt and an elite perimeter defender in Davion Mitchell. And it gave the Knicks offense fits in the first half — they shot just 39.5 percent from the field across the first two quarters.

They were able to largely figure it out in the second half, but were let down by their end-of-game execution.

Last year, the Knicks' NBA Cup triumph was a precursor to their actual championship. They better hope this year's failed Cup run isn't a preview of anything else.

"Obviously disappointed we can't defend the Cup championship," Towns said. "But we still have a lot more games to get ourselves ready to defend the world championship."
